[^F69]5 [^F69]Any person may, on payment of such fee as may be prescribed by an order so made, require the Director to supply to him a copy of or extract from any part of the register, certified by the Director to be a true copy or extract.

[^F69]6 [^F69]Any sums received by the Director under this section shall be paid into the Consolidated Fund.

### Approvals etc. for the purposes of licences

#### 20 Approval of contractors.

[^F70]1 [^F70]Where licences granted under section 7 above include provisions which are framed by reference to the carrying out of relevant operations by persons for the time being approved under this section then, for the purposes of those provisions, persons may be approved under this section in relation to such operations—

[^F70]a [^F70]by the Secretary of State; or

[^F70]b [^F70]with the consent of, or in accordance with a general authorisation given by, the Secretary of State, by the Director.

[^F70]2 [^F70]A person applying for an approval under this section may be required by the person to whom the application is made to comply with such requirements as the person to whom the application is made may think appropriate; and those requirements may include a requirement to satisfy some other person with respect to any matter.

[^F70]3 [^F70]An approval under this section may apply either to a particular person or to persons of a description specified in the approval, and may so apply either in relation to particular relevant operations or in relation to relevant operations of a description so specified.

[^F70]4 [^F70]An approval under this section may specify conditions which must be complied with if the approval is to apply, for any purposes specified in the approval, to any person who is so specified or is of a description so specified; and any such condition may impose on the person to whom the approval is given a requirement from time to time to satisfy any person with respect to any matter.

[^F70]5 [^F70]Nothing in this section shall preclude a person (not being the Secretary of State or the Director) by whom any matter falls to be determined for the purposes of any requirement imposed in pursuance of subsection (2) or (4) above from charging any fee in respect of the carrying out of any test or other assessment made by him.

[^F70]6 [^F70]Any power conferred by this section to give an approval includes power to vary or withdraw an approval given in exercise of that power.

[^F70]7 [^F70]The Secretary of State shall send to the Director—

[^F70]a [^F70]a copy of every approval given by him under this section; and

[^F70]b [^F70]particulars of every variation or withdrawal of an approval so given.

[^F70]8 [^F70]The Secretary of State may by order provide for the charging of fees in respect of the exercise of any functions conferred by or under this section.

[^F70]9 [^F70]Any sums received by the Secretary of State or the Director under this section shall be paid into the Consolidated Fund.

[^F70]10 [^F70]In this section “[^F70]relevant operations[^F70]” means the installation, maintenance, adjustment, repair, alteration, moving, removal or replacement of apparatus which is or is to be connected to any telecommunication system to which a licence under section 7 above relates.

#### 21 Register of approved contractors.

[^F71]1 [^F71]The Director shall keep a register of approvals given under section 20 above at such premises and in such form as he may determine.

[^F71]2 [^F71]The Director shall cause particulars of every such approval, and of every variation or withdrawal of such an approval, to be entered in the register.

[^F71]3 [^F71]Subsections (4) to (6) of section 19 above shall apply for the purposes of this section as they apply for the purposes of that section.

#### 22 Approval etc. of apparatus.

[^F72]1 [^F72]Where licences granted under section 7 above include provisions which are framed by reference to apparatus for the time being approved under this section for connection to telecommunication systems to which the licences relate, then, for the purposes of those provisions, apparatus may be approved for connection to those systems—

[^F72]a [^F72]by the Secretary of State; or

[^F72]b [^F72]with the consent of, or in accordance with a general authorisation given by, the Secretary of State, by the Director.

[^F72]2 [^F72]A person applying for an approval under this section may be required by the person to whom the application is made to comply with such requirements as the person to whom the application is made may think appropriate; and those requirements may include a requirement to satisfy some other person with respect to any matter.

[^F72]3 [^F72]An approval under this section may apply either to particular apparatus or to any apparatus of a description specified in the approval, and may so apply either for the purposes of a particular telecommunication system or for the purposes of any telecommunication system of a description so specified.

[^F72]4 [^F72]An approval under this section may specify conditions which must be complied with if the approval is to apply, for any purposes specified in the approval, to any apparatus which is so specified or is of a description so specified; and any such condition may impose on the person to whom the approval is given a requirement from time to time to satisfy any person with respect to any matter.

[^F72]5 [^F72]Nothing in this section shall preclude a person (not being the Secretary of State or the Director) by whom any matter falls to be determined for the purposes of any requirement imposed in pursuance of subsection (2) or (4) above from charging any fee in respect of the carrying out of any test or other assessment made by him.

[^F72]6 [^F72]Standards to which apparatus of a description specified in the designation must conform if it is to be approved for connection to a telecommunication system so specified or of a description so specified may be designated—

[^F72]a [^F72]by the Secretary of State; or

[^F72]b [^F72]with the consent of, or in accordance with a general authorisation given by, the Secretary of State, by the Director;

[^F72]and a standard so designated may apply subject to such exceptions as may be determined by or under the designation.

[^F72]7 [^F72]A designation under this section may specify conditions which must be complied with if any apparatus of a description specified in the designation is to be regarded, for any purposes so specified, as conforming to the standard to which the designation relates.

[^F72]8 [^F72]Before designating a standard under this section, the Secretary of State or the Director shall give notice—

[^F72]a [^F72]stating that he proposes to make the designation and setting out its effect;

[^F72]b [^F72]stating any conditions which he proposes to specify in the designation; and

[^F72]c [^F72]specifying the time (not being less than 28 days from the date of publication of the notice) within which representations or objections with respect to the proposed designation may be made,

[^F72]and shall consider any representations or objections which are duly made and not withdrawn.

[^F72]9 [^F72]A notice under subsection (8) above shall be given by sending a copy of the notice to the person running the system and such other persons (if any) as the Secretary of State or the Director considers appropriate.

[^F72]10 [^F72]Any power conferred by this section to give an approval or designate a standard includes power to vary or withdraw an approval given or designation made in the exercise of that power.

[^F72]11 [^F72]The Secretary of State shall send to the Director—

[^F72]a [^F72]a copy of every approval given or designation made by him under this section; and

[^F72]b [^F72]particulars of every variation or withdrawal of an approval so given or a designation so made,

[^F72]except where it appears to him requisite or expedient not to do so in the interests of national security.

[^F72]12 [^F72]The Secretary of State may by order provide for the charging of fees in respect of the exercise of any functions conferred by or under this section.

[^F72]13 [^F72]Any sums received by the Secretary of State or the Director under this section shall be paid into the Consolidated Fund.

#### 23 Register of approved apparatus etc.

[^F73]1 [^F73]The Director shall keep a register of approvals given and designations made under section 22 above at such premises and in such form as he may determine.

[^F73]2 [^F73]Subject to subsection (3) below and to any direction given under subsection (4) below, the Director shall cause particulars of every such approval or designation, and of every variation or withdrawal of such an approval or designation, to be entered in the register.

[^F73]3 [^F73]The Director shall not enter in the register particulars of—

[^F73]a [^F73]any approval given or designation made by the Secretary of State under section 22 above a copy of which is not sent to the Director under subsection (11) of that section; or

[^F73]b [^F73]any variation or withdrawal of an approval so given or a designation so made particulars of which are not so sent.

[^F73]4 [^F73]Subsections (3) to (6) of section 19 above shall apply for the purposes of this section as they apply for the purposes of that section.

#### 24 Approval etc. of meters.

[^F74]1 [^F74]Where licences granted under section 7 above include provisions which are framed by reference to meters for the time being approved under this section for use in connection with telecommunication systems to which the licences relate, then, for the purposes of those provisions, meters may be approved for use in connection with those systems—

[^F74]a [^F74]by the Secretary of State; or

[^F74]b [^F74]with the consent of, or in accordance with a general authorisation given by, the Secretary of State, by the Director.

[^F74]2 [^F74]A person applying for an approval under this section may be required by the person to whom the application is made to comply with such requirements as the person to whom the application is made may think appropriate; and those requirements may include a requirement to satisfy some other person with respect to any matter.

[^F74]3 [^F74]An approval under this section may apply either to a particular meter or to any meter of a description specified in the approval, and may so apply either for the purposes of a particular telecommunication system or for the purposes of any telecommunication system of a description so specified.

[^F74]4 [^F74]An approval under this section may specify conditions which must be complied with if the approval is to apply, for any purpose specified in the approval, to any meter which is so specified or is of a description so specified; and any such condition may impose on the person to whom the approval is given a requirement from time to time to satisfy any person with respect to any matter.

[^F74]5 [^F74]Nothing in this section shall preclude a person (not being the Secretary of State or the Director) by whom any matter falls to be determined for the purposes of any requirement imposed in pursuance of subsection (2) or (4) above from charging any fee in respect of the carrying out of any test or other assessment made by him.

[^F74]6 [^F74]Standards to which any meter of a description specified in the designation must conform if it is to be approved for use in connection with a telecommunication system so specified or of a description so specified may be designated—

[^F74]a [^F74]by the Secretary of State; or

[^F74]b [^F74]with the consent of, or in accordance with a general authorisation given by, the Secretary of State, by the Director;

[^F74]and a standard so designated may apply subject to such exceptions as may be determined by or under the designation.

[^F74]7 [^F74]A designation under this section may specify conditions which must be complied with if any meter of a description specified in the designation is to be regarded, for any purposes so specified, as conforming to the standard to which the designation relates.

[^F74]8 [^F74]A meter shall not be approved under this section for use in connection with any telecommunication system unless either—

[^F74]a [^F74]the meter conforms to a standard designated under this section which applies to it for the purposes of that system; or

[^F74]b [^F74]the Secretary of State or the Director is satisfied that, if used in connection with that system in accordance with the approval, the meter would be sufficiently accurate and reliable.

[^F74]9 [^F74]Before giving an approval under this section by virtue of subsection (8)(b) above or designating a standard under this section, the Secretary of State or the Director shall give notice—

[^F74]a [^F74]stating that he proposes to give the approval or make the designation and setting out its effect;

[^F74]b [^F74]stating any conditions which he proposes to specify in the approval or designation; and

[^F74]c [^F74]specifying the time (not being less than 28 days from the date of publication of the notice) within which representations or objections with respect to the proposed approval or designation may be made,

[^F74]and shall consider any representations or objections which are duly made and not withdrawn.

[^F74]10 [^F74]A notice under subsection (9) above shall be given by sending a copy of the notice—

[^F74]a [^F74]in the case of an approval, to the person applying for the approval;

[^F74]b [^F74]in the case of a designation, to the person running the system,

[^F74]and (in either case) to such other persons (if any) as the Secretary of State or the Director considers appropriate.

[^F74]11 [^F74]Any power conferred by this section to give an approval or designate a standard includes power to vary or withdraw an approval given or designation made in the exercise of that power.

[^F74]12 [^F74]The Secretary of State shall send to the Director—

[^F74]a [^F74]a copy of every approval given or designation made by him under this section; and

[^F74]b [^F74]particulars of every variation or withdrawal of an approval so given or a designation so made.

[^F74]13 [^F74]The Secretary of State may by order provide for the charging of fees in respect of the exercise of any functions conferred by or under this section.

[^F74]14 [^F74]Any sums received by the Secretary of State or the Director under this section shall be paid into the Consolidated Fund.

[^F74]15 [^F74]In this section “[^F74]meter[^F74]” means any system or apparatus constructed or adapted for use in ascertaining the extent of telecommunication services provided by means of a telecommunication system.

#### 25 Delegation of functions under sections 22 and 24.

[^F75]1 [^F75]The functions conferred on the Secretary of State by sections 22 and 24 above (other than the powers to make orders) shall be exercisable by any person appointed by the Secretary of State for the purpose to such extent and subject to such conditions as may be specified in the appointment; and an appointment under this section may authorise the person appointed to retain any fees received by him.

[^F75]2 [^F75]Before appointing any person under this section, the Secretary of State shall consult with the persons running the telecommunication systems concerned, or with such organisations as appear to the Secretary of State to be representative of those persons.

#### 26 Grants and loans to persons exercising certain functions.

[^F76]1 [^F76]The Secretary of State may, with the approval of the Treasury, make grants or loans—

[^F76]a [^F76]to persons by whom any matter falls to be determined for the purposes of any requirement imposed in pursuance of subsection (2) or (4) of section 20, 22 or 24 above; or

[^F76]b [^F76]to persons appointed under section 25 above.

[^F76]2 [^F76]Any loans under this section shall be repaid to the Secretary of State at such times and by such methods and interest thereon shall be paid to him at such rates and at such times as he may, with the approval of the Treasury, from time to time direct.

[^F76]3 [^F76]There shall be paid out of money provided by Parliament any sums required by the Secretary of State for making grants or loans under this section.

[^F76]4 [^F76]Any sums received by the Secretary of State under subsection (2) above shall be paid into the Consolidated Fund.

#### 27 Recognition of bodies representing consumers etc.

[^F77]1 [^F77]Where licences granted under section 7 above include provisions which are framed by reference to bodies for the time being recognised under this section to be representing the interests of consumers, purchasers and other users of telecommunication services provided by means of, or telecommunication apparatus connected to, telecommunication systems to which the licences relate, then, for the purposes of those provisions, bodies may be so recognised by the Secretary of State.

[^F77]2 [^F77]A recognition under this section may apply either to a particular body or to bodies of a description specified in the recognition, and may so apply either for the purposes of a particular telecommunication system or for the purposes of any telecommunication system of a description so specified.

[^F77]3 [^F77]The Secretary of State may pay such allowances as he may determine to members of a body recognised by him under this section, and may pay such expenses of a body so recognised as he may determine.

[^F77]4 [^F77]Any power conferred by this section to give a recognition includes power to withdraw a recognition given in the exercise of that power.

[^F77]5 [^F77]There shall be paid out of money provided by Parliament any sums required by the Secretary of State for making payments under this section.

### [[^F78] Standards of performance

#### [^F79]27A Standards of performance in individual cases.

[^F80]1 [^F80]The Director may make regulations prescribing, for any designated operator, such standards of performance in connection with the provision of relevant services by that operator as, in his opinion, ought to be achieved in individual cases.

[^F80]2 [^F80]Regulations under subsection (1) above may only be made—

[^F80]a [^F80]with the consent of the Secretary of State;

[^F80]b [^F80]after consulting—

[^F80]i [^F80]the designated operator; and

[^F80]ii [^F80]persons or bodies appearing to the Director to be representative of persons likely to be affected by the regulations; and

[^F80]c [^F80]after arranging for such research as the Director considers appropriate with a view to discovering the views of a representative sample of persons likely to be so affected and considering the results.

[^F80]3 [^F80]Regulations under this section may—

[^F80]a [^F80]prescribe circumstances in which the designated operator is to inform persons of their rights under this section;

[^F80]b [^F80]prescribe such standards of performance in relation to any duty arising under paragraph (a) above as, in the Director’s opinion, ought to be achieved in all cases;

[^F80]c [^F80]prescribe circumstances in which the designated operator is to be exempted from any requirements of the regulations or this section.

[^F80]4 [^F80]If the designated operator fails to meet a prescribed standard, he shall make to any person who is affected by the failure such compensation as may be determined by or under the regulations.

[^F80]5 [^F80]The making of compensation under this section in respect of any failure to meet a prescribed standard shall not prejudice any other remedy which may be available in respect of the act or omission which constituted that failure.

[^F80]6 [^F80]Any dispute arising under this section or regulations made under this section—

[^F80]a [^F80]may be referred to the Director by either party; and

[^F80]b [^F80]on such a reference, shall be determined by order made—

[^F80]i [^F80]by the Director; or

[^F80]ii [^F80]by such other person as may be prescribed.

[^F80]7 [^F80]Any person making an order under subsection (6) above shall include in the order his reasons for reaching his decision with respect to the dispute.

[^F80]8 [^F80]The practice and procedure to be followed in connection with any such determination shall be such as may be prescribed.

[^F80]9 [^F80]An order under subsection (6) above shall be final and shall be enforceable—

[^F80]a [^F80]in England and Wales and in Northern Ireland, as if it were a judgment of a county court; and

[^F80]b [^F80]in Scotland, as if it were an extract registered decree arbitral bearing a warrant for execution issued by the sheriff.

[^F80]10 [^F80]In this section “[^F80]prescribed[^F80]” means prescribed by regulations under this section.

#### [^F81]27B Overall standards of performance.

[^F82]1 [^F82]The Director may from time to time—

[^F82]a [^F82]determine such standards of overall performance in connection with the provision of relevant services by the designated operator as, in his opinion, ought to be achieved by that operator; and

[^F82]b [^F82]arrange for the publication, in such form and in such manner as he considers appropriate, of the standards so determined.

[^F82]2 [^F82]The Director may only make a determination under subsection (1)(a) above after—

[^F82]a [^F82]consulting the designated operator concerned and persons or bodies appearing to the Director to be representative of persons likely to be affected; and

[^F82]b [^F82]arranging for such research as the Director considers appropriate with a view to discovering the views of a representative sample of persons likely to be affected and considering the results.]

#### [[^F83]27C Information with respect to levels of performance.

[^F84]1 [^F84]The Director shall from time to time collect information with respect to—

[^F84]a [^F84]the compensation made by designated operators under section 27A above; and

[^F84]b [^F84]the levels of overall performance achieved by designated operators in connection with the provision of relevant services.

[^F84]2 [^F84]At such times as the Director may direct, each designated operator shall give the following information to the Director—

[^F84]a [^F84]as respects each standard prescribed by regulations under section 27A above, the number of cases in which compensation was made and the aggregate amount or value of that compensation; and

[^F84]b [^F84]as respects each standard determined under section 27B above, such information with respect to the level of performance achieved by the operator as may be so specified.

[^F84]3 [^F84]A designated operator who, without reasonable excuse, fails to do anything required of him by subsection (2) above shall be liable on summary conviction to a fine not exceeding level 5 on the standard scale.

[^F84]4 [^F84]The Director shall, at least once in every year, arrange for the publication, in such form and in such manner as he considers appropriate, of such of the information collected by or furnished to him under this section as it may appear to him expedient to give to users or potential users of any relevant services provided by designated operators.

[^F84]5 [^F84]In arranging for the publication of any such information the Director shall have regard to the need for excluding, so far as practicable—

[^F84]a [^F84]any matter which relates to the affairs of an individual, where publication of that matter would or might, in the opinion of the Director, seriously and prejudicially affect the interests of that individual; and

[^F84]b [^F84]any matter which relates specifically to the affairs of a particular body of persons, whether corporate or unincorporate, where publication of that matter would or might, in the opinion of the Director, seriously and prejudicially affect the interests of that body.]

#### [[^F85]27D Information to be given to customers about overall performance.

[^F86]1 [^F86]Each designated operator shall, in such form and manner and with such frequency as the Director may direct, take steps to inform those of his customers to whom he supplies relevant services of—

[^F86]a [^F86]the standards of overall performance determined under section 27B above which are applicable to that operator; and

[^F86]b [^F86]that operator’s level of performance as respects each of those standards.

[^F86]2 [^F86]In giving any such direction, the Director shall not specify a frequency of less than once in every period of twelve months.]

#### [[^F87]27E Procedures for dealing with complaints.

[^F88]1 [^F88]Each designated operator shall establish a procedure for dealing with complaints made by his customers or potential customers in connection with the provision by the designated operator of relevant services.

[^F88]2 [^F88]No such procedure shall be established, and no modification of such a procedure shall be made, unless—

[^F88]a [^F88]the designated operator has consulted persons or bodies appearing to him to be representative of customers for whom he provides relevant services; and

[^F88]b [^F88]the proposed procedure or modification has been approved by the Director.

[^F88]3 [^F88]The designated operator shall—

[^F88]a [^F88]publicise the procedure in such manner as may be approved by the Director; and

[^F88]b [^F88]send a description of the procedure, free of charge, to any person who asks for one.

[^F88]4 [^F88]The Director may give a direction to a designated operator requiring the operator to review his procedure or the manner in which it operates.

[^F88]5 [^F88]A direction under subsection (4) above—

[^F88]a [^F88]may specify the manner in which the review is to be conducted; and

[^F88]b [^F88]shall require a written report of the review to be made to the Director.

[^F88]6 [^F88]Where the Director receives a report under subsection (5)(b) above, he may, after consulting the designated operator, direct him to make such modifications of—

[^F88]a [^F88]the procedure; or

[^F88]b [^F88]the manner in which the procedure operates,

[^F88]as may be specified in the direction.

[^F88]7 [^F88]Subsection (2) above does not apply to any modification made in compliance with a direction under subsection (6) above.]

#### [[^F89]27F Disputes about discrimination etc. in fixing charges.

[^F90]1 [^F90]Any dispute, of a kind to which this section applies, between—

[^F90]a [^F90]a person (“[^F90]the customer[^F90]”) who is, or wishes to be, provided with any relevant service by a designated operator, and

[^F90]b [^F90]that designated operator,

[^F90]may be referred to the Director by either party.

[^F90]2 [^F90]This section applies to any dispute as to whether the designated operator—

[^F90]a [^F90]has exercised undue discrimination against the customer in respect of charges applied, or to be applied, in connection with the provision of the service in question;

[^F90]b [^F90]has shown undue preference to any other person in respect of such charges, to the detriment of the customer; or

[^F90]c [^F90]has applied, or proposes to apply, any charge in connection with the provision of the service in question to the customer which is neither specified in, nor determined in accordance with a method specified in, a notice required by a condition of a kind mentioned in section 8(1)(e) above.

[^F90]3 [^F90]Where a dispute is referred to him under this section, the Director, or an arbitrator (or in Scotland an arbiter) appointed by him, shall determine whether the customer’s allegation is well founded and, if it is, make such order as he considers appropriate.

[^F90]4 [^F90]Any person making an order under subsection (3) above shall include in the order his reasons for reaching his decision with respect to the dispute.

[^F90]5 [^F90]No act or omission of a designated operator which is permitted by any condition—

[^F90]a [^F90]relating to any of the matters referred to in section 8(1)(d) above, and

[^F90]b [^F90]included in the licence granted to him under section 7 above,

[^F90]shall be taken to constitute undue discrimination or undue preference for the purposes of this section.

[^F90]6 [^F90]The practice and procedure to be followed in connection with a reference under this section shall be determined by the Director.

[^F90]7 [^F90]An order under this section—

[^F90]a [^F90]may include such incidental, supplemental and consequential provision (including provision requiring either party to pay a sum in respect of the costs or expenses incurred by the person making the order) as that person considers appropriate; and

[^F90]b [^F90]shall be final and—

[^F90]i [^F90]in England and Wales and in Northern Ireland enforceable, in so far as it includes such provision as to costs or expenses, as if it were a judgment of a county court; and

[^F90]ii [^F90]in Scotland, enforceable as if it were an extract registered decree arbitral bearing a warrant for execution issued by the sheriff.

[^F90]8 [^F90]In including in an order under this section any such provision as to costs or expenses, the person making the order shall have regard to the conduct and means of the parties and any other relevant circumstances.]

#### [[^F91]27G Billing disputes.

[^F92]1 [^F92]The Secretary of State may by regulations make provision for billing disputes to be referred to the Director for determination in accordance with the regulations.

[^F92]2 [^F92]In this section “[^F92]billing dispute[^F92]” means a dispute between a designated operator and a customer concerning the amount of the charge which the operator is entitled to recover from the customer in connection with the provision of any relevant service.

[^F92]3 [^F92]Regulations under this section may only be made after consulting—

[^F92]a [^F92]the Director; and

[^F92]b [^F92]persons or bodies appearing to the Secretary of State to be representative of persons likely to be affected by the regulations.

[^F92]4 [^F92]Regulations under this section may provide that, where a billing dispute is referred to the Director, he may either—

[^F92]a [^F92]determine the dispute, or

[^F92]b [^F92]appoint an arbitrator (or in Scotland an arbiter) to determine it.

[^F92]5 [^F92]Any person determining any billing dispute in accordance with regulations under this section shall, in such manner as may be specified in the regulations, give his reasons for reaching his decision with respect to the dispute.

[^F92]6 [^F92]Regulations under this section may provide—

[^F92]a [^F92]that disputes may be referred to the Director under this section only by prescribed persons; and

[^F92]b [^F92]for any determination to be final and enforceable—

[^F92]i [^F92]in England and Wales and in Northern Ireland, as if it were a judgment of a county court; and

[^F92]ii [^F92]in Scotland, as if it were an extract registered decree arbitral bearing a warrant for execution issued by the sheriff.

[^F92]7 [^F92]Except in such circumstances (if any) as may be prescribed by regulations under this section—

[^F92]a [^F92]the Director or an arbitrator (or in Scotland an arbiter) appointed by him shall not determine any billing dispute which is the subject of proceedings before, or with respect to which judgment has been given by, any court; and

[^F92]b [^F92]neither party to any billing dispute which has been referred to the Director for determination in accordance with regulations under this section shall commence proceedings before any court in respect of that dispute pending its determination in accordance with the regulations.

[^F92]8 [^F92]No designated operator may commence proceedings before any court in respect of any charge in connection with the provision by him of any relevant service unless, not less than 28 days before doing so, the customer concerned was informed by him, in such form and manner as may be prescribed by regulations under this section, of—

[^F92]a [^F92]his intention to commence proceedings;

[^F92]b [^F92]the customer’s rights by virtue of this section; and

[^F92]c [^F92]such other matters (if any) as may be so prescribed.]

#### [[^F93]27H Deposits.

[^F94]1 [^F94]Each designated operator shall, with the agreement of the Director, settle criteria by reference to which the operator will determine—

[^F94]a [^F94]whether a customer is required to pay a deposit before being provided with any relevant service; and

[^F94]b [^F94]if so, the amount which he is required to pay.

[^F94]2 [^F94]The criteria may be varied by the designated operator with the consent of the Director.

[^F94]3 [^F94]Before settling, or varying, the criteria the designated operator shall consult persons or bodies appearing to him to be representative of persons likely to be affected.

[^F94]4 [^F94]Except in such circumstances as may be specified in the criteria, no person who is disabled (as defined by the criteria) shall be required to pay a deposit before being provided with any relevant service by the designated operator.

[^F94]5 [^F94]The designated operator shall—

[^F94]a [^F94]prepare a summary of the criteria, with the agreement of the Director;

[^F94]b [^F94]publicise it in such manner as may be approved by the Director; and

[^F94]c [^F94]send a copy of it, free of charge, to any person who asks for one.

[^F94]6 [^F94]The Director may, after consulting the designated operator, direct him to vary the criteria as specified in the direction.

[^F94]7 [^F94]Subsection (3) above does not apply to any variation made in compliance with a direction under subsection (6) above.]

#### [^F95]27I Complaints about deposits.

[^F96]1 [^F96]Any person who is aggrieved by—

[^F96]a [^F96]the decision of a designated operator to require him to pay a deposit before he is provided with a relevant service, or

[^F96]b [^F96]by the amount which he is so required to pay,

[^F96]may refer the matter to the Director.

[^F96]2 [^F96]On any such reference the Director, or an arbitrator (or in Scotland an arbiter) appointed by him, shall consider whether the criteria settled under section 27H above—

[^F96]a [^F96]have been applied correctly, or

[^F96]b [^F96]are inappropriate in the particular case.

[^F96]3 [^F96]If the Director or arbitrator (or arbiter) considers that the criteria have not been correctly applied, or that they are inappropriate in the particular case, he shall, unless the complaint has been withdrawn or it is otherwise inappropriate to proceed, determine—

[^F96]a [^F96]whether the person concerned is to be required to pay a deposit, and

[^F96]b [^F96]if so, the amount which he is to be required to pay,

[^F96]and give the appropriate direction to the designated operator.

[^F96]4 [^F96]Any person giving a direction under subsection (3) above shall include in the direction his reasons for reaching his decision with respect to the complaint.

[^F96]5 [^F96]The practice and procedure to be followed in connection with a complaint under subsection (1) above shall be determined by the Director.

[^F96]6 [^F96]A direction under this section—

[^F96]a [^F96]may include such incidental, supplemental and consequential provision (including provision requiring either party to pay a sum in respect of the costs or expenses incurred by the person giving the direction) as that person considers appropriate; and

[^F96]b [^F96]shall be final and—

[^F96]i [^F96]in England and Wales and in Northern Ireland enforceable, in so far as it includes such provision as to costs or expenses, as if it were a judgment of a county court; and

[^F96]ii [^F96]in Scotland, enforceable as if it were an extract registered decree arbitral bearing a warrant for execution issued by the sheriff.

[^F96]7 [^F96]In including in a direction under this section any such provision as to costs or expenses, the person giving the direction shall have regard to the conduct and means of the parties and any other relevant circumstances.

#### [^F97]27J Disconnections.

[^F98]Where any person has failed to pay any charges in connection with the provision of any relevant service by a designated operator, no power of that designated operator to discontinue the provision of that service shall be exercised against him as respects any amount which is genuinely in dispute.

#### [^F99]27K Enforcement of standards of performance, etc.

[^F100]1 [^F100]Sections 16 to 18 above shall apply in relation to a designated operator as if it were a condition of the licence granted to him under section 7 above that he shall—

[^F100]a [^F100]achieve the standards of overall performance determined in relation to him under section 27B above;

[^F100]b [^F100]take steps to inform those of his customers to whom he supplies relevant services about—

[^F100]i [^F100]those standards, and

[^F100]ii [^F100]the levels of performance which he has achieved as respects those standards,

[^F100]in accordance with section 27D above;

[^F100]c [^F100]comply with—

[^F100]i [^F100]the requirements of section 27E above, and

[^F100]ii [^F100]any direction given by the Director under subsection (4) or (6) of that section;

[^F100]d [^F100]comply with any order made under section 27F(3) above;

[^F100]e [^F100]comply with any direction given by the Director under section 27H(6) above;

[^F100]f [^F100]comply with any direction given under section 27I(3) above; and

[^F100]g [^F100]comply with the requirements of section 27J above.

[^F100]2 [^F100]For the purposes of the application by this section of sections 16 to 18 above, any term of a licence granted under section 7 above which has or which might have the effect—

[^F100]a [^F100]of excepting a designated operator from liability for a contravention of a condition of that licence, or

[^F100]b [^F100]otherwise restricting any such liability,

[^F100]shall not apply in relation to any contravention of the condition mentioned in subsection (1) above.

#### [[^F101]27L Definitions for sections 27A to 27K.

[^F102]1 [^F102]For the purposes of sections 27A to 27K above—

- [^F102]“[^F102]designated operator[^F102]” means any public telecommunications operator designated for the purposes of those sections by order made by the Secretary of State; and

- [^F102]“[^F102]relevant services[^F102]” means—

  - (a) [^F102]any voice telephony service, telephone rental service, directory service, directory information service or facsimile transmission service provided for occupiers of residential or single line premises; and
  - (b) [^F102]any public call box service.

[^F102]2 [^F102]The Secretary of State shall not exercise his power under subsection (1) above to designate a telecommunications operator unless he is satisfied that the operator provides at least 25 per cent. of the voice telephony services supplied within the area in relation to which he is a public telecommunications operator.

[^F102]3 [^F102]For the purposes of this section—

- [^F102]“[^F102]public call box service[^F102]” means a service which is provided by a designated operator and which consists of the provision of telecommunication apparatus which—

  - (a) [^F102]is owned and operated by the designated operator;
  - (b) [^F102]gives access to a voice telephony service; and
  - (c) [^F102]is intended for use by members of the public generally;

- [^F102]“[^F102]directory service[^F102]” means a service which consists of the preparation and provision of a list (which may be made available in separate parts and through different media) of customers of a designated operator which is not arranged by reference to a description of the trades, professions or businesses carried on by those customers;

- [^F102]“[^F102]directory information service[^F102]” has the same meaning as in section 4(3) above;

- [^F102]“[^F102]facsimile transmission service[^F102]” means a telecommunication service for the transmission of electronic signals by a designated operator, over exchange lines provided by him, for the purposes of making a facsimile of a document;

- [^F102]“[^F102]hard wired telephone[^F102]” means a telephone of a kind which can only be connected to a public telecommunication system by means other than—

  - (a) [^F102]the insertion of a plug into a socket; or
  - (b) [^F102]wireless telegraphy;

- [^F102]“[^F102]single line premises[^F102]” means premises which are not residential premises but which are served by a single exchange line provided by the designated operator;

- [^F102]“[^F102]telephone rental service[^F102]” means a service consisting in the hiring out of any hard wired telephone which is capable of emitting or receiving signals which have been, or are to be, conveyed by means of a public telecommunication system run by a designated operator;

- [^F102]“[^F102]voice telephony service[^F102]” means a telecommunication service for the conveyance of speech over exchange lines provided by the designated operator.]

### Marking etc. of telecommunication apparatus

#### 28 Information etc. to be marked on or to accompany telecommunication apparatus.

[^F103]1 [^F103]Where it appears to the Secretary of State expedient that any description of telecommunication apparatus should be marked with or accompanied by any information or instruction relating to the apparatus or its connection or use, the Secretary of State may by order impose requirements for securing that apparatus of that description is so marked or accompanied, and regulate or prohibit the supply of any such apparatus with respect to which the requirements are not complied with; and the requirements may extend to the form and manner in which the information or instruction is to be given.

[^F103]2 [^F103]Where an order under this section is in force with respect to telecommunication apparatus of any description, any person who, in the course of any trade or business, supplies or offers to supply telecommunication apparatus of that description in contravention of the order shall, subject to subsection (3) below, be guilty of an offence and liable—

[^F103]a [^F103]on summary conviction, to a fine not exceeding the statutory maximum;

[^F103]b [^F103]on conviction on indictment, to a fine.

[^F103]3 [^F103]Subsections (4) to (6) of section 5 above shall apply for the purposes of this section as they apply for the purposes of that section.

[^F103]4 [^F103]An order under this section may, in the case of telecommunication apparatus supplied in circumstances where the information or instruction required by the order would not be conveyed until after delivery, require the whole or part thereof to be also displayed near the apparatus.

[^F103]5 [^F103]For the purposes of this section a person exposing telecommunication apparatus for supply or having telecommunication apparatus in his possession for supply shall be deemed to offer to supply it.

[^F103]6 [^F103]In this section and section 29 below “[^F103]supply[^F103]” shall [^F103][[^F104]have the same meaning as it has in Part II of the Consumer Protection Act 1987][^F103].

#### 29 Information etc. to be given in advertisements.

[^F105]1 [^F105]Where it appears to the Secretary of State expedient that any description of advertisements of telecommunication apparatus should contain or refer to any information relating to the apparatus or its connection or use, the Secretary of State may by order impose requirements as to the inclusion of that information, or an indication of the means by which it may be obtained, in advertisements of that description.

[^F105]2 [^F105]Where an advertisement of any telecommunication apparatus to be supplied in the course of any trade or business fails to comply with any requirement imposed under this section, any person who publishes the advertisement shall, subject to subsections (3) and (4) below, be guilty of an offence and liable—

[^F105]a [^F105]on summary conviction, to a fine not exceeding the statutory maximum;

[^F105]b [^F105]on conviction on indictment, to a fine.

[^F105]3 [^F105]Subsections (4) to (6) of section 5 above shall apply for the purposes of this section as they apply for the purposes of that section.

[^F105]4 [^F105]In any proceedings for an offence under this section it shall be a defence for the person charged to prove that he is a person whose business it is to publish or arrange for the publication of advertisements and that he received the advertisements for publication in the ordinary course of business and did not know and had no reason to suspect that its publication would amount to an offence under this section.

[^F105]5 [^F105]An order under this section may specify the form and manner in which any information or indication required by the order is to be included in advertisements of any description.

[^F105]6 [^F105]In this section “[^F105]advertisement[^F105]” includes a catalogue, a circular and a price list.

#### 30 Enforcement provisions.

[^F106]1 [^F106]The Director or a relevant authority shall have power to purchase telecommunication apparatus, and to authorise any of his or their officers to purchase telecommunication apparatus on his or their behalf, for the purpose of ascertaining whether sections 28 and 29 above and orders made under those sections (in this section referred to as “[^F106]the relevant provisions[^F106]”) are being complied with.

[^F106]2 [^F106]The Director shall have power to enforce the relevant provisions and every local weights and measures authority in Great Britain shall have power to enforce those provisions within their area; but nothing in this subsection shall be construed as authorising the Director or a local weights and measures authority to institute proceedings in Scotland for an offence.

[^F106]3 [^F106]In this section “[^F106]relevant authority[^F106]” means—

[^F106]a [^F106]in relation to Great Britain, the Secretary of State or a local weights and measures authority on whom a power to enforce the relevant provisions is conferred by subsection (2) above;

[^F106]b [^F106]in relation to Northern Ireland, the Department of Economic Development for Northern Ireland.

### Rating of telecommunications operators

#### [[^F107]31 Rating in England and Wales.

(1) In Schedule 3 to the [^M3]Local Government Act 1974 (hereditaments to which section 19(1) of that Act applies) for paragraph 5A (hereditaments occupied by British Telecommunications by certain property used for the purposes of telecommunication services) there shall be substituted the following paragraph—

> **5A**
>
> Any hereditament occupied by a telecommunications operator (within the meaning of the Telecommunications Act 1984) by any such property as follows, namely, posts, wires, underground cables and ducts, telephone kiosks and other equipment being property used for the purpose of providing telecommunication services.

(2) Where an order made under the said section 19 applies to hereditaments occupied by a telecommunications operator by any such property as aforesaid, that operator shall be treated, for the purposes of the law relating to rating, as not being a public utility undertaking.

(3) This section extends to England and Wales only.]

#### 32 Rating in Scotland.

(1) In paragraph 5 of Schedule 1 to the [^M4]Local Government (Scotland) Act 1975 (lands and heritages to which section 6(1) of that Act applies and occupied by British Telecommunications by certain property used for the purposes of telecommunication services)—

(a) for the words “British Telecommunications” there shall be substituted the words “ a telecommunications operator (within the meaning of the Telecommunications Act 1984) ”; and

(b) for the words from “not within” to “purposes of” there shall be substituted the words “ , being property used for the purpose of providing ”.

(2) This section extends to Scotland only.

#### [^F108]33 . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . .

### Acquisition etc. of land by public telecommunications operators

#### 34 Compulsory purchase of land in England and Wales.

[^F109]1 [^F109]Subject to subsection (2) below, the Secretary of State may authorise a public telecommunications operator to purchase compulsorily any land in England and Wales which is required by the operator for, or in connection with, the establishment or running of the operator’s system or as to which it can reasonably be foreseen that it will be so required; and the [^F109][^M5]Acquisition of Land Act 1981 shall apply to any compulsory purchase under this section as if the operator were a local authority within the meaning of that Act.

[^F109]2 [^F109]No order shall be made authorising a compulsory purchase under this section except with the consent of the Director.

[^F109]3 [^F109]The power of purchasing land compulsorily under this section includes power to acquire an easement or other right over land by the creation of a new right.

[^F109]4 [^F109]The following provisions of [^M6][^F109][[^F110]the Town and Country Planning Act 1990][^F109] shall have effect in relation to land acquired compulsorily by a public telecommunications operator under this section as they have effect in relation to land acquired compulsorily by statutory undertakers under any other enactment, namely—

[^F109]a [^F109][[^F110]sections 238 to 240][^F109] (use and development of consecrated land and burial grounds);

[^F109]b [^F109][[^F110]section 241][^F109] (use and development of land for open spaces); and

[^F109]c [^F109][[^F110]sections 271 to 274][^F109] (extinguishment of rights of way, and rights as to apparatus, of statutory undertakers).

[^F109]5 [^F109]Where a public telecommunications operator has acquired any land under this section, he shall not dispose of that land or of any interest or right in or over it except with the consent of the Director.

#### 35 Compulsory purchase of land in Scotland.

[^F111]1 [^F111]Subject to subsection (2) below, the Secretary of State may authorise a public telecommunications operator to purchase compulsorily any land in Scotland which is required by the operator for, or in connection with, the establishment or running of the operator’s system or as to which it can reasonably be foreseen that it will be so required; and the [^F111][^M7]Acquisition of Land (Authorisation Procedure) (Scotland) Act 1947 shall apply to any compulsory purchase under this section as if the operator were a local authority within the meaning of that Act and as if this section had been in force immediately before the commencement of that Act.

[^F111]2 [^F111]No order shall be made authorising a compulsory purchase under this section except with the consent of the Director.

[^F111]3 [^F111]The power of purchasing land compulsorily under this section includes power to acquire a servitude or other right over land by the creation of a new right.

[^F111]4 [^F111]The following provisions of [^F111][[^F112]the Town and Country Planning (Scotland) Act 1997][^F111] shall have effect in relation to land acquired compulsorily by a public telecommunications operator under this section as they have effect in relation to land acquired compulsorily by statutory undertakers under any other enactment, namely—

[^F111]a [^F111][[^F112]section 197][^F111] (provisions as to churches and burial grounds);

[^F111]b [^F111][[^F112]section 198][^F111] (use and development of land for open spaces); and

[^F111]c [^F111][[^F112]sections 224 to 227][^F111] (extinguishment of rights of way, and rights as to apparatus, of statutory undertakers).

[^F111]5 [^F111]Where a public telecommunications operator has acquired land under this section, he shall not dispose of that land or of any interest or right in or over it except with the consent of the Director.

#### 36 Compulsory purchase of land in Northern Ireland.

[^F113]1 [^F113]Where a public telecommunications operator proposes to acquire, otherwise than by agreement, any land in Northern Ireland required by him for, or in connection with, the establishment or running of his system or as to which it can reasonably be foreseen that it will be so required, he may, with the consent of the Director, apply to the Secretary of State for an order vesting that land in him and the Secretary of State shall have power to make such an order.

[^F113]2 [^F113]The power of acquiring land compulsorily under this section shall include power to acquire, by the creation of a new right, an easement or other right over land.

[^F113]3 [^F113]Where a public telecommunications operator has acquired any land under this section, he shall not dispose of it except with the consent of the Director.

[^F113]4 [^F113]For the purposes of the acquisition of land by means of a vesting order under this section, Schedule 6 to the [^F113][^M8]Local Government Act (Northern Ireland) 1972 and Schedule 8 to the [^F113][^M9] Health and Personal Social Services (Northern Ireland) Order 1972 are hereby incorporated in this Act subject to the modifications specified in subsection (5) below.

[^F113]5 [^F113]The said modifications are as follows—

[^F113]a [^F113]for any reference in the said Schedule 6 to a council there shall be substituted a reference to the operator;

[^F113]b [^F113]for any reference in either Schedule to the Department there shall be substituted a reference to the Secretary of State;

[^F113]c [^F113]for any reference in either Schedule to the Act or Order in question there shall be substituted a reference to this Act;

[^F113]d [^F113]in paragraph 6(2) of the said Schedule 6, for the words from “the fund” onwards there shall be substituted the words [^F113]“ funds of the operator (in this Schedule referred to as "the compensation fund’) and shall be discharged by payments made by the operator ”[^F113]; and

[^F113]e [^F113]in paragraph 12(2) of that Schedule for the words “the clerk of the council” there shall be substituted the words [^F113]“ such person as may be designated for the purposes of this Schedule by the operator ”[^F113].

[^F113]6 [^F113]The enactments for the time being in force relating to the assessment of compensation in respect of land vested in a district council by an order made under Schedule 6 to the Local Government Act (Northern Ireland) 1972 shall, subject to any necessary modifications, apply in relation to any land vested in a public telecommunications operator by an order made under this section.

[^F113]7 [^F113]In this section “[^F113]land[^F113]” has the meaning assigned to it by section 45(1)(a) of the Interpretation Act (Northern Ireland) 1954.

#### 37 Entry, for exploratory purposes, on land in England and Wales.

[^F114]1 [^F114]A person nominated by a public telecommunications operator and duly authorised in writing by the Secretary of State may, at any reasonable time, enter upon and survey any land in England and Wales, other than land covered by buildings or used as a garden or pleasure ground, for the purpose of ascertaining whether the land would be suitable for use by the operator for, or in connection with, the establishment or running of the operator’s system.

[^F114]2 [^F114][[^F115]Sections 324(8), 325(1) to (5), (8) and (9) of the [^F114][^M10]Town and Country Planning Act 1990][^F114] (which contain supplementary provisions relating to the powers of entry conferred by [^F114][[^F115]section 324(1) to (7)][^F114] thereof) shall have effect in relation to the power conferred by this section as they have effect in relation to the powers conferred by [^F114][[^F115]the said section 324][^F114], subject however to the following modifications, namely—

[^F114]a [^F114]that [^F114][[^F115]section 324(8)][^F114] (which relates to power to search and bore for the purpose of ascertaining the nature of the subsoil or the presence of minerals therein) shall so have effect as if the words “or the presence of minerals therein” were omitted; and

[^F114]b [^F114]that [^F114][[^F115]section 325(1)][^F114] (which requires twenty-four hours’ notice to be given of an intended entry upon occupied land) shall so have effect as if for the words “twenty-four hours” there were substituted the words [^F114]“ twenty-eight days ”[^F114].

[^F114]3 [^F114]Where, in an exercise of the power conferred by this section, any damage is caused to land or to chattels, the operator shall make good the damage or pay to every person interested in the land or chattels compensation in respect of the damage; and where, in consequence of an exercise of that power, any person is disturbed in his enjoyment of any land or chattels, the operator shall pay to that person compensation in respect of the disturbance.

[^F114]4 [^F114][[^F116]Section 118 of the said Act of 1990][^F114] (which provides for the determination of disputes as to compensation under [^F114][[^F116]Part IV of that Act][^F114]) shall apply to any question of disputed compensation under this section.

#### 38 Entry, for exploratory purposes, on land in Scotland.

[^F117]1 [^F117]A person nominated by a public telecommunications operator and duly authorised in writing by the Secretary of State may, at any reasonable time, enter upon and survey any land in Scotland, other than land covered by buildings or used as a garden or pleasure ground, for the purpose of ascertaining whether the land would be suitable for use by the operator for, or in connection with, the establishment or running of the operator’s system.

[^F117]2 [^F117][[^F118]Sections 269(6) and 270(1) to (5), (8) and (9) of the Town and Country Planning (Scotland) Act 1997][^F117] (supplementary provisions as to powers of entry) shall have effect in relation to the power conferred by this section as they have effect in relation to the powers conferred by [^F117][[^F118]the said section 269][^F117], subject to the following modifications, namely—

[^F117]a [^F117]that [^F117][[^F118]section 270(1)][^F117] (twenty-four hours’ notice to be given of an intended entry upon occupied land) shall so have effect as if for the words “twenty-four hours” there were substituted the words [^F117]“ twenty-eight days ”[^F117]; and

[^F117]b [^F117]that [^F117][[^F118]section 269(6)][^F117] (power to search and bore for minerals etc.) shall so have effect as if the words “or the presence of minerals therein” were omitted.

[^F117]3 [^F117]Where, in an exercise of the power conferred by this section, any damage is caused to land or to corporeal moveables, the operator shall make good the damage or pay to every person interested in the land or corporeal moveables compensation in respect of the damage; and where, in consequence of an exercise of that power, any person is disturbed in his enjoyment of any land or corporeal moveables, the operator shall pay to that person compensation in respect of the disturbance.

[^F117]4 [^F117]Any question arising under this section as to the effect of damage or as to the amount of compensation shall, in the case of dispute, be determined by arbitration, and the reference in such an arbitration shall be to a single arbiter to be appointed by agreement between the parties or, in default of an agreement, by the Secretary of State.

#### 39 Entry, for exploratory purposes, on land in Northern Ireland.

[^F119]1 [^F119]A person nominated by a public telecommunications operator and duly authorised in writing by the Secretary of State may, at any reasonable time, enter upon and survey any land in Northern Ireland, other than land covered by buildings or used as a garden or pleasure ground, for the purpose of ascertaining whether the land would be suitable for use by the operator for, or in connection with, the establishment or running of the operator’s system.

[^F119]2 [^F119]Section 40(2) to (5) and (8) of the Land Development Values (Compensation) Act (Northern Ireland) 1965 (which contains supplementary provisions relating to the power of entry conferred by subsection (1) of that section) shall have effect in relation to the power conferred by this section as it has effect in relation to the power conferred by the said subsection (1), subject however to the following modifications, namely—

[^F119]a [^F119]that section 40(2) (which relates to power to search and bore for the purpose of ascertaining the nature of the subsoil or the presence of minerals therein) shall so have effect as if the words “or the presence of minerals therein” were omitted; and

[^F119]b [^F119]that section 40(3)(b) (which requires three days’ notice to be given of an intended entry upon occupied land) shall so have effect as if for the word “three” there were substituted the word [^F119]“ twenty-eight ”[^F119].

[^F119]3 [^F119]Where, in an exercise of the power conferred by this section, any damage is caused to land or to chattels, the operator shall make good the damage or pay to every person interested in the land or chattels, compensation in respect of the damage; and where in consequence of an exercise of that power, any person is disturbed in his enjoyment of any land or chattels, the operator shall pay to that person compensation in respect of the disturbance.

[^F119]4 [^F119]Section 31 of the said Act of 1965 (which provides for the determination of disputes as to compensation under Part III of that Act) shall apply to any question of disputed compensation under this section.

#### 40 Acquisition of land by agreement.

[^F120]1 [^F120]For the purpose of the acquisition by agreement by a public telecommunications operator of land in England and Wales the provisions of Part I of the [^F120][^M11]Compulsory Purchase Act 1965 (so far as applicable) other than sections 4 to 8, section 27 and section 31 shall apply.

[^F120]2 [^F120]For the purpose of the acquisition by agreement by a public telecommunications operator of land in Scotland, section 109(2) of the [^F120][^M12]Town and Country Planning (Scotland) Act 1972 (incorporation of Lands Clauses Acts) shall, with any necessary modifications, apply for the purposes of this Act as it applies for the purposes of that Act.

[^F120]3 [^F120]For the purpose of the acquisition by agreement by a public telecommunications operator of land in Northern Ireland, the Lands Clauses Acts shall be incorporated with this Act except for sections 127 to 133 (sale of superfluous land) and sections 150 and 151 (access to the special Act) of the [^F120][^M13]Lands Clauses Consolidation Act 1845.

#### 41 Purchase of Duchy of Lancaster land.

[^F121]The Chancellor and Council of the Duchy of Lancaster may, if they think fit, agree with a public telecommunications operator for the sale, and absolutely make sale, for such sum of money as appears to them to be sufficient consideration for the same, of any land belonging to Her Majesty in right of the Duchy of Lancaster which the operator seeks to acquire for, or in connection with, the establishment or running of his system.

### Offences

#### 42 Fraudulent use of telecommunication system.

[^F122]1 [^F122]A person who dishonestly obtains a [^F122][[^F123]service to which this subsection applies][^F122] with intent to avoid payment of any charge applicable to the provision of that service shall be guilty of an offence and liable—

[^F122]a [^F122]on summary conviction, to imprisonment for a term not exceeding six months or to a fine not exceeding the statutory maximum or to both;

[^F122]b [^F122]on conviction on indictment, to imprisonment for a term not exceeding [^F122][[^F124]five years][^F122] or to a fine or to both.

[^F122]2 [^F122][[^F125]Subsection (1) above applies to any service (other than a service [^F122][[^F126]such as is mentioned in section 297(1) of the Copyright, Designs and Patents Act 1988)][^F122] which is provided by means of][^F122] a telecommunication system the running of which is authorised by a licence granted under section 7 above.

#### [[^F127]42A Possession or supply of anything for fraudulent purpose in connection with use of telecommunication system.

[^F128]1 [^F128]Subsection (2) below applies if a person has in his custody or under his control anything [^F128][[^F129](other than an unauthorised decoder as defined in section 297A(4) of the [^F128][^M14]Copyright, Designs and Patents Act 1988)][^F128] which may be used for the purpose of obtaining, or for a purpose connected with the obtaining of, a service to which section 42(1) above applies.

[^F128]2 [^F128]If the person intends—

[^F128]a [^F128]to use the thing—

[^F128]i [^F128]to obtain such a service dishonestly, or

[^F128]ii [^F128]for a purpose connected with the dishonest obtaining of such a service,

[^F128]b [^F128]dishonestly to allow the thing to be used to obtain such a service, or

[^F128]c [^F128]to allow the thing to be used for a purpose connected with the dishonest obtaining of such a service,

[^F128]he shall be guilty of an offence.

[^F128]3 [^F128]Subsection (4) below applies if a person supplies or offers to supply anything [^F128][[^F130](other than an unauthorised decoder as defined in section 297A(4) of the Copyright, Designs and Patents Act 1988)][^F128]which may be used for the purpose of obtaining, or for a purpose connected with the obtaining of, a service to which section 42(1) above applies.

[^F128]4 [^F128]If the person supplying or offering to supply the thing knows or believes that the person to whom it is supplied or offered intends or intends if it is supplied to him—

[^F128]a [^F128]to use it—

[^F128]i [^F128]to obtain such a service dishonestly, or

[^F128]ii [^F128]for a purpose connected with the dishonest obtaining of such a service,

[^F128]b [^F128]dishonestly to allow it to be used to obtain such a service, or

[^F128]c [^F128]to allow it to be used for a purpose connected with the dishonest obtaining of such a service,

[^F128]he shall be guilty of an offence.

[^F128]5 [^F128]A person guilty of an offence under this section shall be liable—

[^F128]a [^F128]on summary conviction, to imprisonment for a term not exceeding six months or to a fine not exceeding the statutory maximum or to both, and

[^F128]b [^F128]on conviction on indictment, to imprisonment for a term not exceeding five years or to a fine or to both.

[^F128]6 [^F128]In this section, references to use of a thing include, in the case of a thing which is used to record any data, use of any of the data.]

#### 43 Improper use of public telecommunication system.

[^F131]1 [^F131]A person who—

[^F131]a [^F131]sends, by means of a public telecommunication system, a message or other matter that is grossly offensive or of an indecent, obscene or menacing character; or

[^F131]b [^F131]sends by those means, for the purpose of causing annoyance, inconvenience or needless anxiety to another, a message that he knows to be false or persistently makes use for that purpose of a public telecommunication system,

[^F131]shall be guilty of an offence and liable on summary conviction to [^F131][[^F132]imprisonment for a term not exceeding six months or a fine not exceeding level 5 on the standard scale or both][^F131].

[^F131]2 [^F131]Subsection (1) above does not apply to anything done in the course of providing a [^F131][[^F133]programme service (within the meaning of the Broadcasting Act 1990)][^F131].

#### 44 Modification etc. of messages.

[^F134]1 [^F134]A person engaged in the running of a public telecommunication system who otherwise than in the course of his duty intentionally modifies or interferes with the contents of a message sent by means of that system shall be guilty of an offence.

[^F134]2 [^F134]A person guilty of an offence under subsection (1) above shall be liable—

[^F134]a [^F134]on summary conviction, to imprisonment for a term not exceeding six months or to a fine not exceeding the statutory maximum or to both;

[^F134]b [^F134]on conviction on indictment, to imprisonment for a term not exceeding two years or to a fine or to both.

#### [[^F135]45 Disclosure of messages etc.

[^F136]1 [^F136]A person engaged in the running of a public telecommunication system who otherwise than in the course of his duty intentionally discloses to any person —

[^F136]a [^F136]the contents of any message which has been intercepted in the course of its transmission by means of that system; or

[^F136]b [^F136]any information concerning the use made of telecommunication services provided for any other person by means of that system,

[^F136]shall be guilty of an offence.

[^F136][[^F137]2 [^F136]Subsection (1) above does not apply to any disclosure made—

[^F136]a [^F136]in accordance with the order of any court or for the purposes of any criminal proceedings;

[^F136]b [^F136]in accordance with any warrant, authorisation or notice issued, granted or given under any provision of the Regulation of Investigatory Powers Act 2000;

[^F136]c [^F136]in compliance with any requirement imposed (apart from that Act) in consequence of the exercise by any person of any statutory power exercisable by him for the purpose of obtaining any document or other information; or

[^F136]d [^F136]in pursuance of any duty under that Act of 2000, or under Part III of the [^F136][^M15]Police Act 1997, to provide information or produce any document to the Interception of Communications Commissioner or to the tribunal established under section 65 of that Act of 2000.

[^F136][^F137]3 [^F136]In subsection (2) above “[^F136]criminal proceedings[^F136]” and “[^F136]statutory power[^F136]” have the same meanings as in the Regulation of Investigatory Powers Act 2000.]

[^F136]4 [^F136]A person guilty of an offence under this section shall be liable—

[^F136]a [^F136]on summary conviction, to a fine not exceeding the statutory maximum;

[^F136]b [^F136]on conviction on indictment, to a fine.]

#### 46 Assaults etc. on persons engaged in the business of public telecommunications operator.

[^F138]1 [^F138]A person who—

[^F138]a [^F138]assaults or intentionally obstructs a person engaged in the business of a public telecommunications operator; or

[^F138]b [^F138]whilst in any premises used for the purposes of the business of such an operator, intentionally obstructs the course of business of the operator,

[^F138]shall be guilty of an offence and liable on summary conviction to a fine not exceeding level 3 on the standard scale.

[^F138]2 [^F138]Any person engaged in the business of a public telecommunications operator may require any person guilty of an offence under subsection (1) above to leave premises used for the purposes of that business and, if any such offender who is so required refuses or fails to comply with the requirement, he shall be liable on summary conviction to a further fine not exceeding level 3 on the standard scale and may be removed by a person engaged in that business; and any constable shall on demand remove or assist in removing any such offender.

### [[^F139] Regulations

#### [^F140]46A Powers to make regulations.

[^F141]1 [^F141]Any power under this Part of this Act to make regulations shall be exercisable by statutory instrument.

[^F141]2 [^F141]Any statutory instrument containing regulations made by the Secretary of State under this Part of this Act shall be subject to annulment in pursuance of a resolution of either House of Parliament.

[^F141]3 [^F141]Any such regulations may—

[^F141]a [^F141]provide for the determination of questions of fact or of law which may arise in giving effect to the regulations;

[^F141]b [^F141]make provision regulating (otherwise than in relation to any court proceedings) any matters relating to the practice and procedure to be followed in connection with the determination of such questions;

[^F141]c [^F141]make provision as to the mode of proof of any matter;

[^F141]d [^F141]make provision as to parties and their representation;

[^F141]e [^F141]provide for the right to appear before and be heard by the Secretary of State, the Director and other authorities;

[^F141]f [^F141]make provision as to awarding costs or expenses of proceedings for the determination of such questions, including the amount of the costs or expenses and the enforcement of the awards;

[^F141]g [^F141]provide for anything falling to be determined under the regulations to be determined by such persons, in accordance with such procedure and by reference to such matters and to the opinion of such persons, as may be prescribed by the regulations;

[^F141]h [^F141]make different provision for different cases, including different provision in relation to different persons, circumstances or localities; and

[^F141]i [^F141]make such supplemental, consequential and transitional provision as the Secretary of State or, as the case may be, the Director considers appropriate.

[^F141]4 [^F141]Any such regulations which prescribe a period within which things are to be done may provide for extending the period so prescribed.]

### [[^F142]Appeals

#### 46B Appeals

[^F143]1 [^F143]This section shall apply to the following decisions of the Secretary of State or the Director under this Part taken on or after 20th December 1999—

[^F143]a [^F143]a decision to grant or refuse to grant a licence under section 7;

[^F143]b [^F143]a decision to include within a licence on its grant particular provisions—

[^F143]i [^F143]describing the telecommunication systems authorised to be run under the licence;

[^F143]ii [^F143]describing the connections authorised to be made;

[^F143]iii [^F143]describing the telecommunication services authorised to be provided;

[^F143]iv [^F143]imposing a condition; or

[^F143]v [^F143]applying the telecommunications code to the licensee subject to such exceptions and conditions as may be included in the licence;

[^F143]c [^F143]a decision with regard to the designation under section 9 of a telecommunication system as a public telecommunication system;

[^F143]d [^F143]a decision with regard to the modification of any exception or condition included in a licence as mentioned in section 10(3);

[^F143][[^F144]da [^F143]a decision with regard to the modification under section 12 of a condition of a licence granted under section 7 above to a particular person;]

[^F143]e [^F143]a decision to exercise any power contained in a licence to give a direction or consent or to make any determination;

[^F143]f [^F143]a decision with regard to the enforcement of conditions in a licence;

[^F143]g [^F143]a decision to revoke a licence;

[^F143]h [^F143]a decision with regard to approval of any person, apparatus or meter; and

[^F143]j [^F143]any other decison (other than a decision made under sections 12 to 15) in respect of which the rights or interests of a person running or wishing to run a telecommunication system under a licence or making or wishing to make any connection or providing or wishing to provide any telecommunication service by means of a telecommunication system licensed under this Act are materially affected.

[^F143]2 [^F143]Subject to subsection (5) below, a person aggrieved by a decision to which this section applies may appeal against the decision on one or more of the following grounds—

[^F143]a [^F143]that a material error as to the facts has been made;

[^F143]b [^F143]that there was a material procedural error;

[^F143]c [^F143]that an error of law has been made; or

[^F143]d [^F143]that there was some other material illegality, including unreasonableness or lack of proportionality.

[^F143]3 [^F143]In England and Wales and Northern Ireland, an appeal lies to the High Court, and in Scotland, an appeal lies to the Court of Session.

[^F143]4 [^F143]The court determining an appeal may—

[^F143]a [^F143]dismiss the appeal; or

[^F143]b [^F143]quash the decision,

[^F143]and where the court quashes a decision it may refer the matter to the Secretary of State or the Director (as the case may be) with a direction to reconsider it and reach a decision in accordance with the findings of the court.

[^F143]5 [^F143]No appeal under this section shall be brought unless the leave of the court has been obtained.

[^F143]6 [^F143]An appeal under this section shall be brought—

[^F143]a [^F143]in respect of a decision made under section 16, without unreasonable delay and in any event not later than 42 days from the date on which the Secretary of State or the Director made his decision; or

[^F143]b [^F143]in respect of any other decision to which this section applies, without unreasonable delay and in any event not later than three months from the date on which the Secretary of State or the Director made his decision, or within such other period as may be specified by rules of court.

[^F143]7 [^F143]The effect of a decision to which an appeal under this section relates shall not, except where the court so orders, be suspended in consequence of the bringing of the appeal.

[^F143]8 [^F143]Any proceedings under this section in the Court of Session shall be before the Lord Ordinary.]

## Part III — Other Functions of Director

### 47 General functions.

[^F145]1 [^F145]It shall be the duty of the Director, so far as it appears to him practicable from time to time, to keep under review the carrying on both within and outside the United Kingdom of activities connected with telecommunications.

[^F145]2 [^F145]It shall also be the duty of the Director, so far as it appears to him practicable from time to time, to collect information with respect to commercial activities connected with telecommunications carried on in the United Kingdom, and the persons by whom they are carried on, with a view to his becoming aware of, and ascertaining the circumstances relating to, matters with respect to which his functions are exercisable.

[^F145]3 [^F145]The Secretary of State may give general directions indicating—

[^F145]a [^F145]considerations to which the Director should have particular regard in determining the order of priority in which matters are to be brought under review in the performance of his duty under subsection (1) or (2) above; and

[^F145]b [^F145]considerations to which, in cases where it appears to the Director that any of his functions are exercisable, he should have particular regard in determining whether to exercise those functions.

[^F145]4 [^F145]It shall be the duty of the Director, where either he considers it expedient or he is requested by the Secretary of State or [^F145][[^F146]the Office of Fair Trading][^F145] to do so, to give information, advice and assistance to the Secretary of State or [^F145][[^F146]the Office of Fair Trading][^F145] with respect to any matter in respect of which any function of the Director is exercisable.

### 48 Publication of information and advice.

[^F147]1 [^F147]The Director may arrange for the publication, in such form and in such manner as he may consider appropriate, of such information and advice as it may appear to him to be expedient to give to consumers, purchasers and other users of telecommunication services or telecommunication apparatus in the United Kingdom.

[^F147]2 [^F147]In arranging for the publication of any such information or advice, the Director shall have regard to the need for excluding, so far as that is practicable,—

[^F147]a [^F147]any matter which relates to the private affairs of an individual, where the publication of that matter would or might, in the opinion of the Director, seriously and prejudicially affect the interests of that individual; and

[^F147]b [^F147]any matter which relates specifically to the affairs of a particular body of persons, whether corporate or unincorporate, where publication of that matter would or might, in the opinion of the Director, seriously and prejudicially affect the interests of that body.

[^F147]3 [^F147]Without prejudice to the exercise of his powers under subsection (1) of this section, it shall be the duty of the Director to encourage relevant associations to prepare, and to disseminate to their members, codes of practice for guidance in safeguarding and promoting the interests of consumers, purchasers and other users of telecommunication services or telecommunication apparatus in the United Kingdom.
